# CLAIMS TABLE (queue item F1), flows-audit, 2026-11-16

**Paste source, in one sentence: RUNSHEET-WORDING.md is the only place that holds text to paste into the video, the run sheet or the post, and its table "Status at a glance" gives the approval status of every paste row (the words PASTE, DELETE, HELD, NO PASTE, SUPERSEDED, defined in the table "Status words" below); the "replace with" cells of Tables 1 and 2 below are proposals, and where they differ from RUNSHEET-WORDING.md, RUNSHEET-WORDING.md wins (see the ONE PRECEDENCE RULE below).** Nothing is approved for the screen until the lead marks a row APPROVED; today no row is approved (PASTE means proposed, waiting for APPROVED). The last column of the to-do tables, "done", only means the file was edited, never approved. This file holds the verdicts and the to-do rows. "I" in this file = the flows-audit session that wrote it. For every live row, the section "Live rows: paste source and status" (after Table 2) names the RUNSHEET-WORDING.md rows and their status; the section "What waits on the lead" (after the table "Status words", above "Start here") is the one list of what is missing.

**ONE PRECEDENCE RULE (read first).** RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (RW) holds every string to paste and the status of each string. This file holds the verdicts (one verdict per claim, in the verdict cell of the lane tables) and the to-do rows. Where any wording in this file differs from RW, RW wins and the difference is a defect of this file. Tables 1 and 2 hold no verdicts; a lane row's verdict cell is the only verdict.

**Legend: what PASTE means.** PASTE means PROPOSED, NOT APPROVED: the text is the one to use once the lead marks it APPROVED. Today nothing is approved. The other status words (DELETE, HELD, NO PASTE, SUPERSEDED) are in the table "Status words" below. The word PASTE stays in RW and here because the checking scripts compare the two files by that word. One status per row: the status of a row is the status of the text in its "replace with" cell, as RW gives it; a SUPERSEDED id only names an older RW row for the same sentence and is not a second action; where one place needs two different statuses, the row is split into sub-rows (7b, 8b, 14b and so on), which are not counted as rows.


**Words used the same way in this file and in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (defined once, here).**
- the lead = the Claude session that gives orders and answers and marks rows APPROVED. This file writes "the lead" for it everywhere (lead answer Q25 item 9; real file names such as V7-NUMBERS-lead-read.md keep their own spelling). Any other lead without "the" in front (lead record, lead id, lead key, a made-up lead) = one made-up person who asked for contact. In text meant to be pasted into the video the made-up person is only called lead record, lead ID or lead key, never "the lead".
- owner = the studio owner, the person who films and posts the video. The owner list is Step D only (lead answer Q25 item 7; OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md): the lead sends the owner two messages, each listing leftover made-up contacts by lead key and contact id (the first after the lead's test with the real HubSpot trial account, the second after the take, P4c); after each message the owner deletes the listed made-up contacts in HubSpot by hand (whether the lead lists one contact or several per message is not stated in any file I read: the lead confirms). The person who receives the alert e-mail is "the person who receives the alerts".
- claim = one checked sentence of a file (claim id A-nnn, B-nnn or X-n). The n8n reservation of one lead record is the reservation row (a row in the n8n database: it marks the lead record as taken by one run), and the time a reservation holds is the claim window (720 s for alerts); none of these is ever called just "claim", and the word "claim row" is not used for the reservation any more (quoted plan sentences that still say "claim row" mean the reservation row). "Row N" = row N of Table 1 or Table 2; a row of a lane table is called a lane row; a row of the lead table is called a lead-table row. table = the lead table (the database table mf_leads) unless "Table 1", "Table 2" (the two to-do tables) or "lane table" is written.
- typed answers = the `y`, the 6-digit challenge number shown on screen and the word `end` that the take script asks for. They are NOT on the owner list (lead answer Q25 item 7); who types them at the take is stated in no file I read: BLOCKED, the lead names the person (list "What waits on the lead", WL-1).
- Verdict words (five; counts add to 330): SUPPORTED 103, RELAYED-ONLY 64, OVERSTATED 84, UNSUPPORTED 15, UNTRACED 64 (103 + 64 + 84 + 15 + 64 = 330; run python3 claims-count.py CLAIMS-TABLE.md). This file holds the verdicts. Where a verdict lives: only in the verdict cell (cell 6) of the claim's row in the lane tables (Lane A, Lane B); Tables 1 and 2 have no verdict column, and the list "Verdict of each row's claims" after Table 2 copies the verdicts for every to-do row. RELAYED-ONLY = the lane tables write it "SUPPORTED ONLY BY RELAYED NUMBER": the same verdict, one count (the script maps one to the other). One verdict per row: the verdict of the FIRST phrase of the verdict cell is the verdict and the one counted; a second verdict is written after the words "second verdict, not counted:" and nothing else. When the files do not prove the words, the first phrase is OVERSTATED and a relayed basis goes second. UNSUPPORTED = the files say it is false. UNTRACED = no file to check it against. The word relayed in lower case is not a verdict word: it names a number told by someone else (raw file not seen). The five words are written in capitals only as a verdict (verdict cells, the verdict list, the counts); RELAYED-INDEPENDENT appears only inside quoted old text and is not a verdict. OVERSTATED = partly true; the words say more than the files show. SUPPORTED = a file or flow step we read says so (not run). UNCHECKED = not looked at; it is not a verdict.
- RW, R: and P: = short names of files. RW = RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (the only place for paste text; its row ids are the claim ids). "R:NN" = line NN of VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md (the run sheet). "P:NN" = line NN of PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md (the page statements). "ORDERS.md" always means the flows-audit copy (see the label Q below); RW writes "ORDERS.md" without this qualifier in its lines 29 and 30 and means the same file.
- Times with Z are UTC; IST (UTC + 5:30) follows in the front part, for example 11:20Z = 16:50 IST, 10:23Z = 15:53 IST.
- Labels used in this file (defined once; a label that no file defines is marked "undefined" and is listed in "What waits on the lead", WL-4):

Q-H = the date and hour of the re-pin (answered: the lead sets it, not before the final evidence; hard latest on the NO-GO path Mon 23 Nov 07:00 UTC (Q27 item 6, ORDERS.md line 156); WL-14). ## Status words (one table; the words PASTE, DELETE, HELD, NO PASTE and SUPERSEDED are the five words of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md; today no row is APPROVED)
| word | meaning | which gate comes first |
|---|---|---|
| PASTE | the string is the one to use | only the lead's APPROVED |
| DELETE | remove the old sentence from its file; nothing is pasted in its place (the older word for this status was CUT; RUNSHEET-WORDING.md and this file no longer use it) | none: the delete needs only the lead's confirmation (WL-6) |
| HELD | the row waits for a decision or a check named in its "needs" cell (for the rows B-067, B-091, B-213, B-214, B-215 and B-215b: the check of the take scripts at the re-pin, Q-H); until then its old sentence and its new sentence are not filmed, spoken, shown or posted | first the check or decision, then APPROVED |
| NO PASTE | the old sentence stays or nothing new is written | none |
| SUPERSEDED | do nothing in this row: another row named in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md does the work | none |
| FILE EDIT | not a status: it names a kind of place (a working file that no viewer sees); RUNSHEET-WORDING.md calls the others SCREEN, VOICE, CARD and PAGE. A FILE EDIT row still has one of the five status words above | the status word decides |
One order for every row: (1) the check or decision in the "needs" cell, (2) for the rows that wait for the re-pin check, that check, (3) the lead's APPROVED, last. Read from RUNSHEET-WORDING.md: the section "Status words (defined once ...)" (line 9 today) and the rule HELD in its section "0b. Rows that wait, and what happens by default" (line 113 today). DELETE in a cell is an action, not a status.

## What waits on the lead (the one list; every "lead fills in", "lead sets", "lead to name" or "needs" elsewhere in this file points here)

6. Tags. Every to-do row (Table 1 and Table 2) starts its "note" cell (second column from the right) with ONE tag; a LIVE row also has ONE change marker straight after the tag (what a viewer would see change):
   - [LIVE] = the row has a step to do before the take: words the viewer sees or hears change, or a file the take or the owner reads while filming or preparing changes, or a check must be read first. It may still wait for a check or choice named in its "needs" cell (list "What waits on the lead"). The change marker says what changes on screen.
   - [LIVE, TEXT ONLY] = the same, and the fix only changes or deletes words.
   - Change markers (written after the tag; only LIVE rows and the two OUT rows with a live delete carry one): [CHANGES: caption or card words] = new or changed words in a caption or on the card; [CHANGES: spoken line] = a line the presenter says; [CHANGES: caption or card line deleted] = an old caption or card line is removed and nothing replaces it on screen; [CHANGES: filming only, no words] = a filming requirement, no words change; [CHANGES: file only, viewer sees nothing] = a working file, the run sheet text or the owner steps, never shown; [CHANGES: post text] = the public post.
   - One rule for the cells "replace with" and "other branch" (the INSTRUCTION rule): they hold ONLY text that could be pasted (a proposal for the file text), or DELETE, or "-". Every order to the editor sits in the "note" cell and starts with the capital word INSTRUCTION: (a note sentence never becomes text). Parenthesised remarks such as "(not checked: ...)", "(intended ...)" and bare labels such as T7b or P4a are notes too and never go on screen, in a caption, on the card or in the post. A "replace with" cell is a proposal for the text of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md and is pasted as written only after RUNSHEET-WORDING.md agrees (RUNSHEET-WORDING.md wins). A sentence such as "The post says only: ..." inside a cell is part of the file sentence to paste (it replaces a whole sentence of OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md), not an order. A row whose "replace with" cell is DELETE and whose note says NOTES-ONLY TEXT: the note holds a text for notes, never for the card or the screen.
   - [OUT OF THIS VIDEO] = one meaning only: no scene, caption, card or post of this video depends on the row's replace text, so the take does not wait for it (the sentence belongs to scenes 5 to 11, to recovery-time numbers, to the table screen, or to a document the video does not use; this also holds for row 78 whose approval, WL-12, is for the later rewrite). Keep the row; apply it when that file is next opened or for the later video (earlier is allowed, for example row 48 in step 0c.2 of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md). A DELETE of an old sentence that stands in a file the video uses is a separate, live action: it is listed in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md and in "Live rows: paste source and status" (row 19 is tagged OUT for its replace texts, and its live delete of R:90 is the LIVE sub-row 19b, RW row A-023a; row 25 is tagged OUT and is the delete at P:120, RW row A-073b, with the HELD replace texts in the sub-rows 25b and 25c).

## Quick words (used before the glossary explains them)
- **Take** = the one filmed run of the demo. **Scene** = a numbered part of the video in VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md. **The lead**: defined once at the top of this file. **Lead record** = one made-up sales lead (a fake person who filled in the form) and its row in the lead table; "lead key" and "lead id" = its id. For the made-up person this file says "lead record", "lead id" or "the made-up person". **Re-pin** = ONE event (lead answer Q25 item 6: the check is inside it, there is no separate re-check): the lead re-points the demo to the new core commit and checks the take files (run_real_check_n8n.py, n8n_drive.py, the v4 table file) before the filming; it is a step of the lead before the take, its date is not written in any file I read, and until it is done every sentence marked "lead verifies at re-pin" is the lead's statement, not seen by us. **Archive** = HubSpot's own word for removing a contact; the desk code comment calls it "delete into the recycle bin" (trace-desk-repo.md, crm_hubspot.py:492-499); whether a removed contact can be restored, and for how long, no file says (UNKNOWN); this file says delete for removing a contact and uses archive only when quoting. **Harness** = a test runner program that runs a check from start to end; the old desk harness is run_real_check.py of the desk repository. **Claim**: defined once at the top of this file; the reservation row (the n8n reservation) is never a row of this table.
- **Mailbox words** (lead answer Q25 item 5) = one word per mailbox, so no mailbox has two names; the lead names them at take prep (list "What waits on the lead", WL-5). Until then this file says intake mailbox for the mailbox that receives the demo mails (the only mailbox counted in the proof); other words for it (the mailbox, real mailbox) mean the same mailbox.
- **Recovery** = the n8n flow mf-recovery (one of the four real-v5 flow files) that runs every 30 s, finds a lead record whose run was cut off (n8n stopped on purpose) and either finishes it (the lead record reaches done) or sends an alert mail (the lead record stays unknown or failed and never reaches done). "Recovery time" = the time from stopping n8n to the lead record being done, so it exists only for a lead record that was finished.
- **K1, K2, K3, K3B** = the four test points where n8n is stopped on purpose: K1 after the reservation row is written and before the CRM write; K2 after the CRM write and before the mail step; K3B after `mail_sent` is set and before the mail is delivered; K3 after the mail was delivered and before the table update (mf.py:151-164; the full list of test labels is in the glossary under "Test and kill labels").
- **P checks** (P1, P2, P4a, P4b, P4c) = five checks run on made-up lead records (listed under "Checks"): P2, P4a and P4b before the take, P4c after the take, P1 only for the later video. P4a = what the real CRM answers with for a contact's number; P2 = whether n8n saves its runs. **D1 to D3** = three choices of the lead (under "Choices the lead must make before the take": D1 settled, D2 moot, D3 open with a default); they are not the numbered decisions. **Q4 to Q8** = the lead's answers 4 to 8 in ORDER 013 of flows-audit/ORDERS.md; "17:05Z" = the lead's answer timed 17:05 UTC (22:35 IST) in that order (its date is not recorded in the file); that answer says: no recovery number on screen that the data does not support.
- **Table 1 / Table 2** = the two to-do tables below (the T numbers T1 to T21 are test names, not tables). **Lane A / lane B** = the two reading passes (lane A: the video files; lane B: plans, notes, READMEs; each lane has ids A-nnn or B-nnn). **Claim** = a sentence checked; **reservation row** = the n8n database row that marks a lead record as taken by one run (never the same thing). **Check** = a P check; **test** = a test run.
- **`id` / `vid`** = the two field names in which HubSpot may send back a contact's number. **`mail_sent`** = a column of the lead table set to true BEFORE the mail step starts, so it means "the mail step was started", not "the mail arrived".
- **F-008, F-001, F-01** = F-008 and F-001 are findings in FINDINGS.md (F-008: the flows save no runs; F-001: the lead table definition lacks the alert columns); F-01 (two digits) is a different finding from another file, cited at AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md:20. **B-205** = a lane B row about the typed challenge number. **The pause** = the wait in the take script while the contact list is filmed, ended by the typed word `end` (who types it: BLOCKED, list "What waits on the lead", WL-1). **Queue item F1 / C4** = this table (F1) and the rewrite of the owner steps as a new file version 4 (C4), both in QUEUE.md. **The 8 flow files** = the four real-v5 flow files (mf-form, mf-hook, mf-recovery, mf-sub-crm-mail) plus the same four of film-v5 (the stand-in set, out of this video); only the four real-v5 files matter here. **Stand-in** = our own small test program in place of a real service. **Sweep** = one pass of the 30 s alert check.
- **Flow sets and test words, defined once** (other places point here): **stand-in** = our own small test program in place of a real service; **film-v5** = the flow set that uses stand-ins (later video, not this one); **real-v5** = the four flow files that use the real mail service and the real CRM; this is the take. **Loopback re-smoke** = a short trial run on the take machine through its own internal address before the take (whether it still exists is open: list "What waits on the lead", WL-10). **Re-smoke** = any such trial run of the whole take set-up; "the first smoke run" = the earlier one that showed SQLite (DECISION-one-store.md line 8).
- Other words with one meaning here:
  - **v3, v4, v5** always name flow sets, never a version of a document (a version of a document is written out: "version 4 of the owner steps"; OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md is version 3 of the owner steps and has nothing to do with the v3 flows). v3 = the older flows of the V7 test series (lease 20 s). v4 = the n8n-flows folder and n8n-flows-v4 (lease 120 s) and real-v4 and film-v4 (lease 45 s). v5 = n8n-flows-real-v5 and n8n-flows-film-v5 (lease 45 s); real-v5 is the take's set.
  - **lane3** = an earlier test series by another session.
  - **P** and **R** in the lane tables are file keys (PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md, VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md), not the P checks.
  - **Claim** = a checked sentence; the 720 s claim window and the reservation row are always named in full.
  - **Tick** = write x in the done column.
  - **Stage** = the model-call program; OutStage = a step name inside a flow.
  - **OwnsClaim1** = a flow step that checks this run still holds the claim.
  - **hot, warm, cold** = the three labels the model step may give a lead (step LabelOk).
  - **WP1, WP3b, WP4** = numbered work packages of PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md.
  - **KS n=2** = a kill series of 2 runs.
  - **PUB** = n8n-published-numbers.md.
  - **Cloud session, demo session, builder, reviewers** = the earlier AI sessions that wrote the lane tables (cloud session = the one without the laptop files) and the fresh AI reviewer sessions; no row is individually marked reviewed, the reviewers sampled rows.
  - **Scene numbers and "FINAL scene 4"** = VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md numbers its scenes 1 to 12: scenes 1 to 4 are this video, scenes 5 to 11 are out (scene 11 is optional), scene 12 is the closing card (answered, item 8 in "Start here"). It also has a FINAL block that repeats the scene 4 caption. No row here describes scene 11.
- Review notes are in the folder handover/flows-audit/reviews-f1/ and in the files handover/flows-audit/review-claims-r5-*.md. A read-only trace of the public desk repository is in handover/flows-audit/reviews-f1/trace-desk-repo.md.

**What this file is.** It checks the sentences that the video script, the page statements, the plans and the owner steps say about what the n8n flows do. Each sentence (a "claim") was traced once to a file or flow step and got a verdict; reviewers then re-read a sample of the rows, not all of them. The two to-do tables hold 95 of the 330 claims in 87 rows (a row can cover several claims when one replacement text fixes them all, for example row 13 covers A-041, A-042 and A-097; and five rows cover no claim id); a claim id with no row has no replacement text; most rows give replacement text, but a row that says DELETE or "keep" gives none. The other 235 claims (330 - 95) have no row in these two tables: 101 are SUPPORTED, 63 RELAYED-ONLY, 53 UNTRACED and 18 OVERSTATED (derived from the count of line "Count (by script)" below, 2026-11-16). Rule: a SUPPORTED claim without a row needs no change, but SUPPORTED only means that a file says so (not run); a sentence is spoken, shown or posted only if a PASTE row of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md holds it. Leave out of the video every RELAYED-ONLY, UNTRACED and OVERSTATED sentence among the 235. The eighteen OVERSTATED ones: A-002, A-015 and A-049 have live deletes in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (rows A-002, A-016c, A-016e: R:4, R:32, R:41 line 1); B-003 has its rows B-003a, B-003b and B-003d there; A-096 and B-155 have a paste row there (rows A-096 and B-155); A-003 and B-107 have a NO PASTE row there (rows A-003 and B-107; later video, not this take); A-024 and B-102: leave the sentence about how many alerts out; A-010 and A-045 have their rows there too (A-010, A-010b, A-010c, A-045), and A-074, B-086, B-126 and B-140 have NO PASTE rows there (same fact as A-010: what n8n keeps of the request body; no replacement text); B-032 and B-056 have rows there. Nothing here was run; all verdicts come from reading files, so treat them as "the files say so", not as test results. "Checked in text" in this file always means that the file or flow step was read, never run.

**What failed or was not checked (read this first).**
- Nothing ran. No test was run in n8n (the workflow tool), Postgres (the lead-table database: the database that holds one row per lead record), HubSpot (the CRM, meaning the customer list) or a mail server. All data is made up.
- 64 of the 330 claims are RELAYED-ONLY: they rest on a number that another session sent us and whose raw file is not in this checkout. One summary of those numbers is present (handover/demo/V7-NUMBERS-lead-read.md, the lead's own reading); the raw laptop files are absent.
- 64 claims are UNTRACED: the file or step (a "node", one step inside a flow file) that would prove or disprove them is not in this checkout, so nothing can be said either way. UNSUPPORTED is different: the files we have contradict the claim or show it false (15 claims). Do not film or post an UNTRACED or UNSUPPORTED claim as fact. Most of the 64 RELAYED-ONLY and 64 UNTRACED claims have no replacement text (the exceptions are the claims among the 12 added on purpose, listed under "Count"): leave such a sentence out. The lane A ones (lane A = the claims from the two video files, see the glossary) that touch the video are listed under "Before filming: claims to leave out" below. 17 claims moved out of UNTRACED after a read-only trace of the public desk repository (see "What the desk repository trace shows" below): 11 to SUPPORTED (B-017, B-018, B-049, B-064, B-065, B-066, B-068, B-069, B-089, B-090, B-158), and 6 to OVERSTATED (B-016, B-019, B-067, B-088, B-091, B-092); the verdict counts already include them. B-038 was first moved to unsupported and returned to untraced in round 7 (PLAN-4:66 is plan wording about a module not yet built, the same class as B-045 and B-046).
- The five verdict words add up to all 330 claims: SUPPORTED 103 + RELAYED-ONLY 64 + OVERSTATED 84 + UNSUPPORTED 15 + UNTRACED 64 = 330 (OVERSTATED = partly true; the words say more than the files show). Word meanings and the rule of one verdict per row: top of this file.
- HubSpot (the real CRM) was never tested by us. Whether it answers with the id HubSpot gives each contact as an all-digit `id` or as `vid` is not checked (ID-VID-CHECK.md in this folder). If it answers anything else, the lead record fails and no mail is sent (read in the flow text, not run). No scene may say "the real CRM answered with an id" until check P4a is read.
- n8n runs on its own database today (a small SQLite file). This rests on one earlier smoke run (a first trial run of the whole take set-up; DECISION-one-store.md line 8), not on a check of the take machine. "One database" must stay out of the video unless check P4b (listing the take machine's n8n data folder) shows no SQLite file after a new trial run (a re-smoke); whether that trial run happens is open: BLOCKED, the lead decides (WL-10).
- After the take (decision 2): the take's HubSpot identity is read-only and the take never deletes contacts. The made-up contacts stay in HubSpot; the lead lists them by lead key and contact id and the owner deletes them by hand in the HubSpot user interface (check P4c). Older files say otherwise: OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md line 103 (and the notice at line 95) say the script archives them, PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md line 111 says archive_all runs in a finally block, AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md line 20 says the code deletes them (its F-01 is a code fix whose file, review-codebase-1.md, is not in this checkout). ORDERS.md line 82 (flows-audit/ORDERS.md, not demo/ORDERS.md) says they stay unless the F-01 fix lands (so it is conditional); decision 2 and lead answer Q13 (flows-audit/ORDERS.md line 110) say it without condition. The public desk repository (read-only trace) shows that its script run_real_check.py DOES archive the contacts of its run at the end when it is started with the option --cleanup (default off): crm_hubspot.py:492-499 sends the HubSpot delete call, run_real_check.py:1101-1107 calls it. The take does not use that path. Lead answer Q13 (ORDERS.md, 10:23Z = 15:53 IST): the take uses run_real_check_n8n.py in take mode plus n8n_drive.py, not the desk script's archive_all path; in the take the HubSpot identity is read-only and cleanup is OFF (result.json archive_contacts 'no'); leftovers are listed once by lead key and contact id and the owner deletes them by hand. The old desk harness still deletes; the take does not. Lead verifies at re-pin: the take scripts are not in this repository, so this is the lead's statement, not seen by us. The take scripts (run_real_check_n8n.py, n8n_drive.py) are not in this repository, so B-214 and B-215 stay UNTRACED, and B-067 and B-091 are OVERSTATED (they say the take's run archives). B-017 is SUPPORTED as a description of the old desk harness. Rows 32, 33 and 48 and the lane rows give the same text (round 8); B-214 gets no wording, only a HELD, DO NOT QUOTE marker, because what the take script does at a wrong or closed answer is not seen by us.
- Money risk: a paid model call could happen by accident. Two rows (A-004 and B-071, both UNTRACED: the stage program is not in any file we have) say the stage program builds the desk's live model client from the environment, so a take-day stage may be able to call the paid model. The desk code that was read has no "fake" switch: only the address in DESK_LIVE_BASE_URL decides whether the model is the paid one or a local fake (config.py:193-216, constants.py:170), so the risk is real for the desk code; treat it as a risk, not a fact about the stage. Cost line: up to 3 live model calls under the owner's standing credits yes (FACTS-ALREADY-DONE.md, owner answers 2026-11-15); the lead states the ceiling for up to 3 sends from the ledger at take prep; the owner is not asked for a new number (lead answer Q25 item 1); no US$0 claim. No live call before that ceiling is stated.
- Not checked: the verdicts that no review note names (folder reviews-f1; they are first-pass readings), the relayed numbers, FINDINGS.md and the patches. Lane A read the built flow JSON, but the reviewers sampled only about 45 cites and did not re-check every flow node. Of the 103 SUPPORTED claims, 17 are marked "text-only, needs run" (A-011, A-013, A-029, A-034, A-043, A-065, A-066, A-067, A-071, B-005, B-009, B-053, B-060, B-082, B-129, B-152, B-167): the node path exists but what n8n, HubSpot or the mail server really does was never observed (11 more were moved to SUPPORTED by the desk repository trace: the code or document says so, not observed; the other 75 are plain text facts such as a name, a setting or a SQL line); none of the 103 was confirmed by a run.

Row 10 has the same new words in both outcomes of P1; only the later rewrite waits for P1.

**Words with two meanings, fixed here.** "Lead record" = one made-up sales lead (a fake person who filled in the form) and its row in the lead table. "The lead" = the Claude session defined at the top of this file; every other "lead" (lead id, lead key, lead record) means the made-up person, and in pasted text the made-up person is never called "the lead". "Lease" = how long a claim on a lead record lasts before another run may take it (45 s in the real-v5 flows; older flow sets used 20 s and 120 s, see the glossary). Letters and numbers that look alike, kept apart: "WL-n" = an item of the list "What waits on the lead"; "D1 to D3" = three choices of the lead; "decision N" = item N of DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md; "Step D" = the owner's one step; "lane A" and "lane B" = the two reading passes; "A-nnn" and "B-nnn" = claim ids; "queue item C4" = the rewrite of the owner steps. None of these is a list label. "Intake mail" = the mail the flow sends about one lead record to the intake mailbox (quoted notes call it "the lead mail").

**Who decides what.**
- The lead settled D1 (decision 1); D2 is moot; D3 is open with a default (choices, below). The lead reads the result of every check (P1, P2, P4a, P4b, P4c), and approves each wording.
- The owner (the person who films and posts the video) does only Step D, listed under "Owner's actions" below.
- The agent (the flows-audit session) edits the files named in the "file to edit" column, only after the lead has settled the choice, answer or check named in the "needs" column. A row whose "needs" says a check runs after the take (P4c) is not held back: its text is safe now and the check only confirms it afterwards. Rows tagged OUT OF THIS VIDEO do not hold up the take (tag rule in "Start here", item 6). No target file has been edited yet: this table is the to-do list.
- The laptop agent (the session on the take laptop) runs the checks P2, P4a, P4b (and P1 for the later video) on made-up lead records; the lead reads the result.

**Owner's actions, in the order of work.** The owner list is Step D only (lead answer Q25 item 7; details in OWNER-STEPS-q16-rows.md, which replaces the older OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md list):
1. Step D: the lead sends the owner two messages, each listing leftover made-up contacts by lead key and contact id (the first after the lead's test with the real HubSpot trial account, the second after the take, P4c); after each message the owner deletes the listed made-up contacts in HubSpot by hand (whether the lead lists one contact or several per message is not stated in any file I read: the lead confirms); the take never deletes contacts and the owner types nothing else (check P4c). Step A (a new money yes with a US$ amount) no longer exists: the standing yes for the credits already exists (FACTS-ALREADY-DONE.md, owner answers 2026-11-15) and the lead states the ceiling for up to 3 sends from the ledger at take prep; the owner is not asked.
2. Replaced, no longer on the owner list (lead answer Q25 item 7): typing y, the 6-digit challenge number and end; the take-day laptop settings; cancelling the passwords made only for the take; revoking the model key (decision 5). Who does each of these is stated in no file I read: BLOCKED, the lead names the person (list "What waits on the lead", WL-1, WL-2). They are not owner steps.

**Order of work (agent and lead).** This is the only order; no other list in this file overrides it.
1. D3 is the only open choice among D1 to D3 (lead; default: keep the flow's text, see D3 below). D1 is settled for this video by decision 1 (no recovery time in a caption); D2 is moot (the caption figures are cut); D3 matters only for out rows.
2. First the lead confirms the Gmail filter and label `demo` (older owner sheet, step 4, no longer an owner step) and the HubSpot property `hadavin_label` (older owner sheet, step 6, no longer an owner step): rows 1 and 4 wait for both and for P4a. Then run and read P4a (rows 1, 4, 7, 39 and the CRM clause of row 79 wait for it), then P2 (row 6 only; row 16 does not wait for P2), then P4b (row 36). The laptop agent runs them, the lead reads them. P1 is for the later video only; the rows that need it are out.
3. Work the live rows of Table 1 (tags [LIVE] and [LIVE, TEXT ONLY]) top to bottom (the table is grouped by file, so one visit per file) and tick the "done" column: rows 1 to 9, 14 to 17, 22, 32, 33, 36, 39, 79, 85, 86 and 87, with the sub-rows 7b, 8b and 33b to 33d (rows 14 and 15 are live deletes of old card lines since round 7: lead answers Q15 and Q19, RUNSHEET-WORDING.md). Table 2 and the out rows of Table 1 wait for the later video or for the next time their file is opened.
4. Before filming, go through "Before filming: claims to leave out" below.
5. After the take: P4c (count what is left in HubSpot; row 33 is already safe text before the take).

**Glossary (the labels and words used in this file; a few are used above before they are explained here).**
- Files read. Lane A (run sheet and page statements): VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md and PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md. Lane B (plans, notes, READMEs), 14 files: PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md, WATCH-CHECKLIST.md, DOUBTFUL-SCENE.md, FAILED-TAKE-NOTE.md, RUN-ON-LAPTOP.md, RERUN-RUNBOOK.md, CLEAN-MACHINE-1.md, N8N-START.md, DESIGN-A-README.md, V5-README.md, n8n-owns-vs-desk-owns.md, n8n-skip-on-conflict.md, n8n-published-numbers.md, OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md. V5-README.md exists as two identical copies (real-v5 and film-v5), which is why some notes say 15 files.
- Lane A and lane B are the two reading passes below. Lane A ids are A-001 to A-101 (A-056 unused, so 100 claims; A-099, A-100 and A-101 are the claims of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md rows A-016f, A-016b and A-016g). Lane B ids are B-001 to B-226. X-1 to X-4 are four extra lane B rows from decision files that are in force but are not among those 14 files (AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md lines 6, 7, 16 and DECISION-one-store.md line 16).
- Claim = one sentence a file says about what the flows do. Row = one line of a table (a "table row" is a row of the lead table). "Take" = the filmed run of the demo. "Scene" = a numbered part of the video in VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md. "Card" = a title text on screen; "caption" = a line of text under a picture; "crop list" = the numbered list at VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md:87 of what must be cropped or hidden on screen. "Check" = only one of the checks P1 to P4c ("sweep" = one pass of the 30 s alert step of the recovery flow); "tick" = write an x in the done column.
- Verdicts: SUPPORTED = a node or file text we read says so; not run. Where the verdict cell says "text-only, needs run" (older rows: "run-dependent"), the node path exists but what n8n, HubSpot or the mail server really does was never observed. RELAYED-ONLY (the lane tables write it "SUPPORTED ONLY BY RELAYED NUMBER") = true only if a figure sent to us by another session is right. OVERSTATED = partly true; the words say more than the files show, or more than a lead answer allows (A-097). UNSUPPORTED = the files we have contradict it or show it false (a claim with no node and no contradiction is UNTRACED). UNTRACED = the code or finding that would prove it is not in this checkout, so it cannot be checked here; treat as not safe to film or post as fact. A verdict cell that names a second part after the first phrase is counted by its first phrase (rule of one verdict per row, top of this file; the second part is written after "second verdict, not counted:"). STALE (used only in Part 5b) = an owner step that FACTS-ALREADY-DONE.md says is already done; it is not a verdict and is not counted. In lane rows B-093 to B-095 and B-130 the word "stale" says the sentence is out of date; their verdict is UNSUPPORTED. Same claim, different lanes: "lease plus 25 to 30 s" is OVERSTATED (by relay) everywhere now; "n8n keeps the request" is OVERSTATED in both lanes (A-010, A-045, A-074, B-086, B-126, B-140: no file we read shows what n8n keeps; the relayed T7b/HB171 reading is written after "second verdict, not counted:" and is not a number): do not state it as measured.
- D1 to D3: three choices of the lead (D1 settled, D2 moot, D3 open with a default; listed below under "Choices the lead must make before the take"; not the numbered decisions of DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md): D1 = whether a recovery time goes on screen; D2 = confirming the per-kill-point medians; D3 = what to do about the words "A person decides" in the alert mail. Checks: P1 (later video only), P2, P4a, P4b, P4c (below; there is no P3: it was an old name for P4a and was dropped). P1 = does the mail step end in error or done when the stand-in drops the connection; P2 = is run saving ON and fix F-008 applied; P4a = what the real CRM answers (see `id` and `vid` below); P4b = does n8n keep its own SQLite file; P4c = leftover HubSpot contacts after the take (run after the take). Q4 to Q8: the lead's answers 4 to 8 to our open questions, given in ORDER 013 of ORDERS.md (its Q9 accepted demotions of findings and touches no claim), which are summarised under "What breaks the lead's answers". "17:05Z" = the lead's answer timed 17:05 UTC (22:35 IST; the date is not given in the file): put no recovery number on screen that the data does not support. "IF plus alert" (answer Q7) = the IF step (a yes/no split in a flow) plus the alert mail, which the run sheet describes only after the video. "Stand-in drop" = the test where our own stand-in mail program drops the connection after taking the mail (scenes 7 to 10). "Branch" = the alternative text for the case where a check fails. F-nnn (three digits) = finding number in FINDINGS.md (F-003, F-004 and F-008 are proposed patches, not applied; F-001 = the lead table definition file lacks the alert columns; F-008 = no error workflow, and the flows save no runs, so its patch switches saving on for the take). "F-01" (two digits) = finding 1 of a different file, review-codebase-1.md, cited in AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md:20; that file is not in this checkout, so F-01 and F-001 are different findings.
- Test and kill labels (the names used by mf.py, the test runner in the demo folder; mf.py:151-164): K1, K2, K3, K3B = tests where n8n is stopped on purpose. K1 = after the reservation row is written, before the CRM write. K2 = after the CRM write, before the mail step. K3B = after mail_sent is set, mail not yet delivered. K3 = after the mail was delivered, before the table update. The older lane3 test series used other K numbers, and VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md:24 and PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md:20 map them differently (A-022): a per-kill figure is tied to the K names above only by the relay, so the rows that give per-kill figures (rows 25, 27, 28; A-085, A-086) must be confirmed against the lane3 report before use. T1 = ten leads sent at once (a "burst"; FINDINGS.md F-046 notes that "T1" also names an "empty key" test of the audit). M0 = the same lead sent twice (and an invalid e-mail); M1 = three bursts of ten identical requests (mf.py:119-138). T8 = the model step refuses or fails. T15 = the CRM answers HTTP 500. T12 = a dropped mail connection. T14 = an idle stack (the set of running programs of one test). T17 = the stand-in CRM answers with `id` only (expected: done, numeric crm_id); T18 = the CRM answers with neither id nor vid (stand-in only; V5-README.md:20, mf.py:339 and :348). T19 to T21 = three new tests proposed by this audit (FINDINGS.md:21): same key twice sends one mail; empty or invalid key gives 422; Postgres down gives 503 and a resend works (in that order); none was run. T7b = a test about what n8n keeps of the request. AF1 = the alert-only mail refusal test (the stand-in answers 451 twice). AF2 = a hung mail server test, not run.
- Earlier test series by other sessions: lane3 (older film flows, 18 kill runs at lease 45 s), V7 (v3 flows, lease 20 s), HB numbers (ids of runs inside those series). Raw files are absent. "Relayed" = a number sent to us, raw file not seen. "V7 lead-read" = V7-NUMBERS-lead-read.md, the lead's own reading of the V7 numbers (the file name keeps the old word). Flow sets and leases: v3 = the older flows of the V7 series (lease 20 s); v4 = the n8n-flows folder and n8n-flows-v4 (lease 120 s) and real-v4 / film-v4 (lease 45 s; the 18 lane3 kill runs ran on film-v4); v5 = n8n-flows-real-v5 and n8n-flows-film-v5, the take's flows (lease 45 s). "v2" in a name can also mean the second version of the table screen (n8n-table-screen-v2) or the v2 text of DESIGN-A-README.md.
- Parts of the system: "desk" = the studio's earlier lead-desk program (its public repository is not in this checkout; a read-only trace of it is in reviews-f1/trace-desk-repo.md). "Stage" = the small program that makes the model call (qualify). qualify_stage.py = the planned stage file, absent here; stage_server.py = the prototype that is here; qs_01 = a planned test of it. "Stand-in" = our own small test program in place of a real service. "Real" flows use real mail and HubSpot; "film" flows use stand-ins. v2 to v5 = versions of flow sets (see the flow-set note above). "Stack" = the set of programs running for one test (n8n, database, stand-ins); a "reused" stack was started earlier and used for earlier runs, a "fresh" one was just built. "Harness" = a test runner program with its own work folder (the demo harness runs mf.py; the lane3 harness is a separate one). "Variants a to e" (in n8n-published-numbers.md) = test set-ups of n8n's duplicate protection, not the take's flows. "Lane A" and "node" are defined above. Node names (SetCrmId, CrmIdOk, OwnsClaim1, CrmUpsert, SendMail, MailClaim and so on) are step names inside the flow files.
- File keys for the lanes: R = VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md; P = PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md; and the other names in the index by file below. "R:30" means file R, line 30. Line numbers are as the files stand on 2026-11-16: if a line has moved, find the text by its words.
- Other terms: "reservation row" = the n8n database row that marks a lead record as taken by one run (older text: "claim row"); "needs" column = the decision or check a row waits for (none = nothing); "replace with" = the exact new text, or DELETE; "other branch" = the text to use when the check fails; "note" = an instruction that is not part of the new text; "done" = tick when the file is edited. A "-" in a cell = empty; "needs: none" = nothing to wait for.
- More labels: `id` and `vid` = the two field names in which HubSpot may send back a contact's number (`vid` is the older one); the flow accepts either, but only if the value is all digits (CrmIdOk). RELAYED-INDEPENDENT = a tag in PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md: the lead relayed what the separate lane3 test program printed; the raw lane3 reports were not found. RELAYED-LEAD = a tag in the same file: the lead relayed a pass from the laptop V4-par run; the result file was not read. UNBACKED = a tag in the same file: a statement with no run, no definition or no named file; do not publish it. `mail_sent` = a column of the lead table; the flow sets it to true BEFORE it sends the mail (A-044), so `true` means "the mail step was started", not "the mail arrived" (rows 7 and 13 and the "unknown" story of scenes 7 to 10 use the column that way). "Window" = the 720 s alert claim window (the time an alert, once claimed, is not taken again) or the money "window cap" of the live model calls (PLAN-4:137, ends 2026-11-23); the text says which. "Verdict" = the audit's verdict (above) or the `verdict` field in the flow's reply (new, done, unknown and so on); the text says "reply verdict" for the second. "Done" = the state `done` of a lead record, or the last column of the tables.

**Count (by script).** 330 claims read, of which 266 were traced to a file or flow step and 64 could not be traced (UNTRACED) (lane A 100, lane B including X-1 to X-4 230): SUPPORTED 103, RELAYED-ONLY 64, OVERSTATED 84, UNSUPPORTED 15, UNTRACED 64 (lane A: 21, 23, 39, 6, 11; lane B and X: 82, 41, 45, 9, 53). Of the 103 SUPPORTED claims, 17 say "text-only, needs run" in the verdict cell (A-011, A-013, A-029, A-034, A-043, A-065, A-066, A-067, A-071, B-005, B-009, B-053, B-060, B-082, B-129, B-152, B-167); 11 more (B-017, B-018, B-049, B-064, B-065, B-066, B-068, B-069, B-089, B-090, B-158) were moved from UNTRACED by the desk repository trace and say "the code or doc says so, not observed"; the other 75 are text facts, and none of the 103 was confirmed by a run. The tables list 95 claims in 87 rows: Table 1 has 45 rows (22 LIVE, of which 9 are LIVE, TEXT ONLY, and 23 OUT OF THIS VIDEO) and Table 2 has 42 rows (all OUT), so 22 rows are live and 65 are out; the sub-rows are not counted although some carry the tag LIVE. Five rows carry no claim id (row 36, which is in no lane, row 78, a sentence to add, and rows 85, 86 and 87, take preparation added by answer Q16), so 82 rows carry the 95 claims: the 82 claims whose verdict first word is OVERSTATED or UNSUPPORTED and that have a row (84 + 15 = 99, minus the 18 named next = 81), plus 14 others added on purpose (A-050, A-099, B-017, B-024, B-038, B-045, B-046, B-051, B-078, B-183, B-189, B-196, B-214, B-215; B-196 moved from UNSUPPORTED to UNTRACED but keeps its row 73), 95 claims in all. The 18 OVERSTATED claims with no to-do row are A-002, A-015 and A-049 (their live DELETE is in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md rows A-002, A-016c and A-016e: R:4, R:32, R:41 line 1), B-003 (its rows B-003a, B-003b, B-003d and B-219 are in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md), A-096 and B-155 (paste rows A-096 and B-155 in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md), A-003 (SUPERSEDED by N-2 there) and B-107 (HELD there), A-010 and A-045 (the same replacement string as rows A-010 and A-010b of RUNSHEET-WORDING.md; A-045 has its own SUPERSEDED row there), B-032 and B-056 (rows B-032 and B-056 there), A-024 and B-102 (leave the sentence about how many alerts out; no replacement text), A-074, B-086, B-126 and B-140 (NO PASTE rows of the same ids in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md: the old sentence stays, nothing is written; same fact as A-010). A-077 has its two rows 23b and 23c. Sub-rows 7b, 8b, 14b, 15b, 15c, 19b, 23b, 23c, 25b, 25c, 27b, 28b, 33b, 33c, 33d, 35b and 48b belong to the rows with the same number and are not counted. To re-check, run `python3 handover/flows-audit/claims-count.py handover/flows-audit/CLAIMS-TABLE.md`; it reads every row that starts with `| A-nnn |`, `| B-nnn |` or `| X-n |`, checks 7 cells and 8 pipe signs per row (11 cells in the two to-do tables), takes the first verdict phrase of cell 6 (longest phrase first), and prints the counts and any offending row (it prints 87 to-do rows). Re-run it after any edit. The 99 here (84 + 15) counts the four X rows too (X-1 UNSUPPORTED, X-2 OVERSTATED, X-4 UNSUPPORTED, X-3 UNTRACED); RUNSHEET-WORDING.md section 6 and its checker (tools/check_runsheet_paste.py) count only A and B ids, so they report 96 (99 minus X-1, X-2, X-4) flagged and 63 (64 minus X-3) UNTRACED. 99 against 96 and 64 against 63 are the same claims counted with and without the X rows, not a disagreement.


## Legend
1. Verdicts: see the glossary above.
2. File keys in the lanes, part 1: R = demo/VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md; P = demo/n8n-flows/PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md; PLAN = demo/PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md; DOUBTFUL = demo/DOUBTFUL-SCENE.md; FAILED = demo/FAILED-TAKE-NOTE.md; RUN = demo/RUN-ON-LAPTOP.md; WATCH = demo/WATCH-CHECKLIST.md; CM = demo/CLEAN-MACHINE-1.md; RERUN = demo/RERUN-RUNBOOK.md; SKIP = demo/n8n-skip-on-conflict.md.
3. File keys, part 2: START = demo/n8n-flows/N8N-START.md; DESIGN = demo/n8n-flows/DESIGN-A-README.md; V5 = demo/n8n-flows-real-v5/V5-README.md (same text in film-v5); OWNS = demo/n8n-owns-vs-desk-owns.md; PUB = demo/n8n-published-numbers.md; ONS = flows-audit/inputs/OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md; AGENT-SCOPE = flows-audit/inputs/AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md; DECISION = flows-audit/inputs/DECISION-one-store.md. The tables below use the real file name and the line as the file stands today.
4. The last three columns of each table: "other branch" (third from the end) = what to use if the check fails; "note" (second from the end) = an instruction that is not part of the new text; "done" (last) = tick when the file is edited. Pipe signs inside cells are written as a slash, so a quoted sentence may show a slash where the file has a pipe sign.

## Choices the lead must make before the take (D1 to D3): D1 settled, D2 moot, D3 open with a default
- D1: put a recovery time on a caption or card? Settled for this video by decision 1: no. Scene 4 shows no timer (Q25 item 12). Rows 25, 27 and 28 (out of this video) give per-kill figures sent to us; row 3 gives a planning-only figure of about 61 s that never goes on screen. The K1-only alternative wording (K1, 5 runs, median 60.8 s, max 61.9 s; relayed, raw not seen, measured on stand-in film-v4 flows, not on real-v5) is Decided out by decision 1 (Q14, 2026-11-16).
- D2 (moot): confirm the final per-kill medians (K1 60.8 s and max 61.9 s; K2 60.9 s and max 61.2 s). PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md lines 151 and 152 say the final medians were not re-relayed. Moot for the video: the caption figures are Decided out by decision 1 (Q14, 2026-11-16).
- D3 (open; the default applies unless the lead decides otherwise): the alert mail's own text says "A person decides", which a viewer may read as an approval step. Default: keep the flow's text and change only the spoken line and captions (row for A-036); the on-screen mail still shows those words. Alternative: change the alert text in both recovery flow files and rebuild (more work, removes the words from the screen).

## Checks (P1, P2, P4a, P4b, P4c)
P2, P4a and P4b run before the take, P4c after it, P1 only for the later video (the rows that need P1 are out of this video).
- P1 (later video only; it is not part of the take's rehearsal): VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md:64 lists it as rehearsal step 3, but the video of this file never runs it. What it is: the laptop agent runs the stand-in mail drop (our own stand-in mail program on the film-v5 flows) five times. Is it cancelled? ORDER 037 item 3 (ORDERS.md line 177) says one filled flow set with the real trial-account credential in every phase and drops the stand-in HubSpot swap; it does not name P1, so the lead confirms (list "What waits on the lead", WL-9); the take does not wait for it. The lead reads whether the mail step ended in error or done. If it ended in error: use the main text of rows marked P1. If it ended done, or if the five runs do not all end the same way (the lead may change this rule): use the "other branch" text; the lead also rewrites scenes 7 to 10, because the lead record then ends done with no alert, not unknown.
- P2: F-008 applied, the 4 real-v5 flow files rebuilt, saving ON confirmed. If not: use the "other branch" text of the P2 rows (drop the run list from scene 1; no claim about what n8n keeps).
- P4a (the id HubSpot gives each contact): with ONE made-up lead, open the CrmUpsert output in the n8n execution list and read (a) which field names are present (`id`, `vid`, both or neither), (b) number or text, (c) digits only; then confirm the table row has a `crm_id` of digits only and state `done`. Only then may a scene say the real CRM answered with an id. If it fails, the flow text says the lead record ends failed after its tries with no mail (3 tries by the flow text, not run) (details in ID-VID-CHECK.md section B). Nobody has done this yet. If P4a fails the take is postponed: failed rows are never filmed, and the rows that say the real CRM answered (A-008a and others) stay conditional on P4a.
- P4b (n8n database): list the files in the take's n8n data folder; if a SQLite file (database.sqlite and its -shm, -wal files) is there, n8n keeps its own records in it, and the video says only "n8n keeps its own records". The first smoke run did use SQLite (DECISION-one-store.md line 8). Only a listing with no SQLite file after a new trial run (a re-smoke; WL-10) allows "one database"; the P4b listing itself needs no re-smoke.
- P4c (clean-up): after the take, count the made-up contacts left in HubSpot (the contact property hadavin_lead_id starts with L). The take never deletes contacts (decision 2). The owner deletes them by hand in the HubSpot user interface and the lead gives the list by lead key and contact id. Claims that say the take's script "archives" or "deletes" the contacts are wrong for the take (lead answer Q13, 10:23Z = 15:53 IST; lead verifies at re-pin; The old desk harness still deletes; the take does not.).
- Q22 item 3 (answered yes by the lead, ORDERS.md line 134, 11:30Z; one status: answered, applied): before scene 2 the lead preparation note says that the take uses a lead id and an e-mail address never used before (not one used in a rehearsal, in P4a or in a trial run of the whole set-up), that mf_leads has 0 rows for the lead key (saved select), and that the intake mailbox is emptied or filtered, because the line that the mailbox shows 1 mail depends on it. Still open: who runs the select (BLOCKED, list "What waits on the lead", WL-3), and whether a loopback re-smoke still exists (WL-10: ORDER 037 item 3, ORDERS.md line 177, says one filled flow set in every phase and does not use the word loopback; the NOTE of 14:35Z in ORDERS.md still names a short loopback re-smoke).
- Q21 (proposed; the lead decides; text only): the lead may do an optional clean-up of the take's own database after filming; it is written nowhere in the video, and the take itself still deletes nothing (Q19-1, decision 2). Missing input: the lead's yes or no.

## What the desk repository trace shows (read-only; reviews-f1/trace-desk-repo.md)
The public desk repository (duplicate-proof-lead-desk-n8n, checkout e720998; every cited file is identical at f3c39cf) was read, not run. It was written against a smaller script than PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md describes. Its own page docs/real-services.md:7 says it was never run against a real HubSpot account or a real Gmail mailbox, so SUPPORTED from this trace means "the code or doc says so", never "observed". Findings that need the lead before the take:
- The old desk script (run_real_check.py) archives (deletes into the recycle bin) a run's contacts only when started with --cleanup. The take does not use it: lead answer Q13 (10:23Z = 15:53 IST) says the take uses run_real_check_n8n.py in take mode plus n8n_drive.py, cleanup OFF. The take scripts are not in this repository: the lead verifies them at re-pin.
- The desk mail counter counts by the header X-Send-Key, not by subject, and the n8n flow sets no such header (row 82), so it cannot count the flow's mails as it stands.
- The desk makes lead ids as L plus 12 hex characters (constants.py:52-53, :293); PLAN-4 says L plus 16 hex. If desk validation is reused, 16 hex fails.
- The desk's call caps count per desk process (row 81); exit code 1 is wider than the run sheet says (row 80).
- Wrong cites in the source notes (not edited here, the files are outside this table): PLAN-4 "run_real_check.py 2292-2298" is really run_real_check.py:993, :572-581, :1101-1107 and crm_hubspot.py:451-470; "870-880" is really :572-581; "761-833" is really :463-537; "imap_count.py 120-189" is really :127-136, :169-190, :213-228; "live_claude.py 517-531" is really :505, :521-523, :318-340; "load_driver.py:111" is really :123; AGENT-SCOPE-DO-AND-DONT.md:20 "crm_hubspot.py:504-509" is really :492-499 (the delete call is on :497).

## Words used
- R = handover/demo/VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md. P = handover/demo/n8n-flows/PAGE-STATEMENTS-V4.md. BM = handover/demo/n8n-mainframe/build_mainframe.py. RV5 = handover/demo/n8n-flows-real-v5/. FV5 = handover/demo/n8n-flows-film-v5/. SUB = mf-sub-crm-mail.json, REC = mf-recovery.json, FORM = mf-form.json (HOOK = mf-hook.json is the same flow with other field names).
- Verdicts: SUPPORTED = a node or SQL does exactly this in the take files (read only; "text-only, needs run" in the verdict cell, and the older "run-dependent", mean the node path exists but what n8n really does was never observed). SUPPORTED ONLY BY RELAYED NUMBER = a number from a run nobody here saw; the source is named. OVERSTATED = partly true; the words say more than the files show. UNSUPPORTED = a finding or the files we have say the opposite or show it false. UNTRACED = I could not trace it (the code or finding that would prove it is absent), reason given. A claim with no node and no contradiction is UNTRACED. (definitions: see the glossary line)
- lane3, V7, HB-numbers = earlier test runs by other sessions; their raw files are not in this repository (FINDINGS NOT COVERED item 5).
- The real flows use a HubSpot node (RV5 SUB CrmUpsert, type n8n-nodes-base.hubspot) and placeholders for addresses; the film flows use an HTTP call to a stand-in CRM (FV5 SUB CrmUpsert, type httpRequest, url $env.MF_CRM_URL). Verified.

## Node facts checked in text, used below (quoted from the built JSON or BM)
- Lease: `interval '45 seconds'` occurs once in each of mf-form, mf-hook, mf-recovery (ClaimStuck) in both v5 folders, 0 in SUB (grep count). n8n-flows-v4 has 120 (F-004).
- Claim (FORM): `INSERT INTO mf_leads(...) ... ON CONFLICT (lead_key) DO NOTHING RETURNING lead_key, claim_token, state;` alwaysOutputData true, no onError. Then `Claimed` (IF lead_key notEmpty): true -> IsProcessing -> SubInput -> RunSub (waitForSubWorkflow true) -> ReplyRan; false -> Stored (`SELECT state FROM mf_leads WHERE lead_key=$1;`) -> ReplyStored (`repeat: true`).
- ReplyRan verdict: `{ done: 'new', unknown: 'unknown', stage_failed: 'processing', lost_claim: 'processing' }[$json.outcome] || 'processing'`.
- SUB: `MailClaim` = `UPDATE mf_leads SET mail_sent=true ... WHERE lead_key=$1 AND claim_token=$2 AND mail_sent=false AND state='processing' RETURNING lead_key;` then `SendMail` (retryOnFail false, onError continueErrorOutput). Output 0 -> MarkDone; output 1 -> MarkUnknownA. If MailClaim returns nothing -> MarkUnknownB (`... AND mail_sent`). Mail subject `={{ '[demo] lead ' + <lead key> }}`.
- REC: Every30s (secondsInterval 30) -> ClaimStuck (`state NOT IN ('done','review','unknown','failed') AND claimed_until < now() AND attempts < 3 ... LIMIT 5`) -> StuckRow -> RunSub; MarkFailed (`state=CASE WHEN mail_sent THEN 'unknown' ELSE 'failed' END ... attempts >= 3`); AlertClaim (`alert_until=now() + interval '720 seconds' ... state IN ('unknown','failed') AND alert_sent=false`) -> AlertRow -> AlertSend -> (output 0) AlertMark / (output 1) AlertBackoff (`interval '60 seconds' * LEAST(alert_attempts, 5)`). Swallow-error nodes (continueRegularOutput): ClaimStuck, RunSub, MarkFailed, AlertClaim, AlertBackoff, AlertMark.
- Alert text: subject `'[demo] Lead desk: mail outcome unknown for ' + lead_key` (unknown) or `'... lead failed ' + lead_key`; body `'Lead id ' + key + ': the mail may or may not have been sent. A person decides.'` or `'... could not be finished after 3 tries. A person decides.'`. Film recipient `alerts@example.test`; real recipient `$env.DESK_ALERT_TO`.
- Saving: all 8 files `saveDataSuccessExecution: none`, `saveDataErrorExecution: none`; `errorWorkflow` absent (0 hits). Slack: 0 hits in all 8 files. Approval or audit: 0 hits in the 8 files.
- Table screen v2 ReadLeads: `SELECT lead_key, state, attempts, mail_sent, alert_sent, updated_at FROM mf_leads ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 50;` = 6 columns. No DELETE statement in any of the 8 files (grep).
- n8n-flows/mf_leads.sql (the file named for the current flows) ends at `updated_at` and has NO alert columns (F-001, checked in text); v5 folders ship no SQL.
- smtp_catcher.py: the mail is appended to its file (line 40 block) BEFORE the `.drop` branch (`if drop > 0: ... return`, no "250 queued"). `.rcptfail` answers 451 to a named recipient (AF1). crm_stub.py `fail_status` makes every call answer an error (the scene 5 switch). mf.py: K3B = "killed after mail_sent was set ... mail NOT yet delivered" (line 158); K3 = "killed after the mail was delivered" (line 164).

## Sentences that break the lead's answers (ORDER 013 Q4 to Q8, and 17:05Z)
- Q4 ("the video says nothing about one database; default 'n8n keeps its own records'"): grep for "one database" and "single database" in R and P: 0 hits. No break. Caveat: A-010, A-053 and A-074 use "n8n data"; plain wording "n8n keeps its own records" is the lead's default, use it.
- Q5 (neither "approval" nor "audit trail" in the video; scoped claim is "In this recording, the same lead ID sent twice gave one e-mail message in the intake mailbox." (Q25 item 2)): grep for "approv" in R and P: 0 hits; "audit trail" 0 hits; the word "audit" appears only as "audit advises cut" (R:40, R:77), meaning the review, not on screen. No break. AT RISK: "A person decides" is on screen in the alert text (A-036, REC AlertSend) and in closing card line 2 (A-050); F-042 asks for "the flow does not record the decision" next to it. Not a banned word, but a viewer may read it as an approval step; there is none.
- Q6 (scene 5 says "a stand-in CRM"; "HubSpot" only for real-v5 scenes): BREAKS: R:4 (A-001, "real CRM service in scenes 1 to 5"); R:30 card (A-008, "real ... CRM services" not limited to scenes 1 to 4); R:86 caption (A-026, says "the real CRM cannot be made to refuse", the words "a stand-in CRM" are not in it). "HubSpot" appears only in R:31 and R:58 inside "must not claim" and risk cells, never as screen text: OK.
- Q7 (invalid e-mail: sentence in the run sheet before the video; the IF plus alert after): PENDING under FIX-PLAN item 5 (F-031, Q7 answered: the sentence goes in before the video; the lead applies it; the candidate wording is row 78 per Q19 item 4, also N-3 in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md), not yet in R or P at the time of reading; P:33 (A-057) says every lead that "could not be finished" is alerted, which is false for `review` (REC AlertClaim and ClaimStuck both exclude `review`).
- Q8 (do not run the table screen; use a rehearsal picture or the Postgres node preview for scene 10): BREAKS: R:62 step 1 tells the laptop agent to "Import and activate the table screen flow"; R:39 and R:88 still plan to show it "if it worked first time"; README of v2 says "NOT run in n8n" and v1 "was run live ... and works" (F-023 conflict).

## Part 2: key and abbreviations
Paths are under `handover/demo/` unless stated. RV5 = n8n-flows-real-v5, FV5 = n8n-flows-film-v5, BM = n8n-mainframe/build_mainframe.py (generator; the JSON matches it, checked by parsing both folders), SUB = mf-sub-crm-mail.json, FORM = mf-form.json, REC = mf-recovery.json, PLAN = PLAN-4-NLITE-FROM-LEAD.md, ONS = flows-audit/inputs/OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md.
Verdicts: SUPPORTED (read in the node or SQL), UNSUPPORTED (the files in this repository contradict it or show it false), OVERSTATED (partly true; says more than the node does), UNTRACED (the code that would prove it is not in the repository), SUPPORTED ONLY BY RELAYED NUMBER (a run-time fact; source file named).
Skipped on purpose: stage directions, install and start commands, hour estimates, calendar rows, status lines, click paths. A cell "-" means no rewording needed.

Facts used for verdicts (all read in the files):
- Claim (FORM, RV5 and FV5): `INSERT INTO mf_leads(lead_key,email,name,state,claimed_until,claim_token,attempts) VALUES ($1,$2,$3, CASE WHEN $4::boolean THEN 'processing' ELSE 'review' END, now() + interval '45 seconds', gen_random_uuid()::text, ...) ON CONFLICT (lead_key) DO NOTHING RETURNING lead_key, claim_token, state;` ; the INSERT text is in n8n-flows-real-v5/mf-form.json:97; table key `lead_key text PRIMARY KEY` (n8n-flows/mf_leads.sql:4).
- MailClaim (SUB): `UPDATE mf_leads SET mail_sent=true ... WHERE lead_key=$1 AND claim_token=$2 AND mail_sent=false AND state='processing' RETURNING lead_key;`. SendMail: retryOnFail false, onError continueErrorOutput, subject `'[demo] lead ' + lead_key`, options only `appendAttribution false`.
- CrmIdOk (SUB, v5): `/^[0-9]+$/.test(String(CrmUpsert.id ?? CrmUpsert.vid ?? ''))`; false goes to OutStage.
- REC: ClaimStuck (`state NOT IN ('done','review','unknown','failed') AND claimed_until < now() AND attempts < 3 ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 5`), MarkFailed (`CASE WHEN mail_sent THEN 'unknown' ELSE 'failed'`), AlertClaim (`alert_until=now() + interval '720 seconds'`), AlertMark (`alert_sent=true ... AND alert_token=$2`), AlertMark and ClaimStuck and MarkFailed and RunSub onError continueRegularOutput; link order from the timer: MarkFailed, ClaimStuck (then RunSub), AlertClaim (BM:115).
- Real HubSpot node (BM:25-30): `operation upsert`, `email` from SubIn, custom properties hadavin_lead_id and hadavin_label; comment in BM:26 says the node upserts keyed on the e-mail and answers `{vid, isNew}`; no timeout option; retry 2, error output.
- Env reads: RV5 `$env.DESK_URL`, `DESK_TIMEOUT_MS`, `DESK_ALERT_TO`; FV5 `MF_STAGE_URL`, `MF_STAGE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MF_CRM_URL`. No other `$env` in the 8 files.
- Diff real-v4 against real-v5 (parsed JSON): added CrmIdOk; changed CrmUpsert (credential id) and SetCrmId; form, hook, recovery files identical. Film: added CrmIdOk, changed SetCrmId.

## Part 4: sentences that break the lead's answers (Q4 to Q8; Q9 touches no claim)

Rules: (R1) the video does not say "one database" (default: "n8n keeps its own records"); (R2) no "approval" and no "audit trail" words (scoped claim: "In this recording, the same lead ID sent twice gave one e-mail message in the intake mailbox.", Q25 item 2); (R3) scene 5 says "a stand-in CRM", "HubSpot" only for real-v5 scenes.

R1, "one database": none of the 14 files says it. Not a break. But DECISION-one-store.md:14 ("ONE database for everything we own") is a decision in force, and N8N-START.md:4 ("separate from n8n's own SQLite") says the opposite. Whichever stands, the video must use "n8n keeps its own records" until the move to Postgres is proven (the decision calls itself "an AIM, not a state", DECISION:23, :26).

R2, "approval" and "audit":
- n8n-owns-vs-desk-owns.md:5 "ambiguous sends marked for a person, approval, its own counts" (B-183). Breaks R2.
- n8n-owns-vs-desk-owns.md:26 "the portal, approval of changed repeats" (B-189). Breaks R2.
- PLAN-4:50 "Pass rule, result file, audit lines" (B-024). Word "audit". Internal plan table, not video text; reword before any reuse.
- PLAN-4:154 "The spend ledger and the stage journal stay as audit (keys and labels only)." (B-059). Word "audit". Reword.
- Near misses (no banned word, but the same idea): the alert text "A person decides." (BM:109; DOUBTFUL:12, B-078; F-042 gives the safe wording); DECISION-one-store.md:16 "Audit = the lifecycle rows of `mf_leads` (state and timestamps)" breaks R2 and contradicts F-043 (one row per lead, state overwritten, no order of events). Not one of the 14 files, but a decision in force.
- "Single-dependency audit" at PLAN-4:57 uses "audit" in a different sense (design review); fine, but keep it out of the video text.

R3, stand-in versus HubSpot:
- PLAN-4:57 and ONS:12, :131 "n8n writes the CRM contact" (B-027, B-203, B-219) has no scene qualifier. It is true only for the real-v5 flows. For scene 5 (film flows, SMTP stand-in with `.drop`) the CRM is the stand-in (FV5 CrmUpsert is an HTTP call to `crm_stub`); say "a stand-in CRM".
- DOUBTFUL-SCENE.md does not mention a CRM or HubSpot at all (checked). Not a break.
- n8n-flows-real-v5 and film-v5 READMEs say "HubSpot" only about the real build or the credential id. Not a break.
- WATCH-CHECKLIST.md:10 names HubSpot; it describes the older desk run, not scene 5.

Other claims the lead's answers touch:
- The broad sentence "the same lead id sent twice is processed once" is stated in PLAN:9, :57, ONS:131 and :135 (ONS:131 is replaced as part of the whole post sentence, row B-219) (PLAN:184 states only the narrow claim and is not replaced) and is SUPPORTED for the repeat path only (B-003) and is REPLACED at those four places by the wording of rows B-003a, B-003b, B-003d and B-219 in RUNSHEET-WORDING.md (not kept); it is NOT used in the video (Q25 item 2: 12 of 20 and 2 of 5 runs, B-195). The one claim the video keeps is the scoped sentence of row A-016f: "In this recording, the same lead ID sent twice gave one e-mail message in the intake mailbox."
- Other copies of "processed once" (round 12z search of handover/demo and flows-audit/inputs): VIDEO-RUNSHEET.md:4, :32 and :41 are the rows A-002, A-016c and A-016e (live DELETE, replaced only by the scoped sentence of A-016f). OWNER-STEPS-realrun-v3.md:131 and :135 are B-219 and B-003d. demo/n8n-kill-between-steps.md:11 says one outbox call of the old stand-in workflow sk-k stands for the work done once; it is a description of a stand-in test, not a claim of the video, and is not pasted anywhere (no row). inputs/plan-wp6-resmoke-v5.md lines 14, 398, 399, 476 and 505 use the words processed once only inside the limit "does NOT hold for a crash between SMTP send and MarkDone", which agrees with the scoped sentence (no row). inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md:2 (decision 1) still calls the narrow claim "a lead sent twice with the same id is processed once"; the lead corrected that in answer Q25 item 2 and that file is the lead's, not edited here: the lead should add the scoped sentence of A-016f to decision 1 (next step, owner of the file: the lead).
- "Exactly 1 alert", "ONE alert" (DOUBTFUL:12, V5:4, DESIGN:15, :32, RUN-ON-LAPTOP:63): OVERSTATED (B-077, B-150, B-173).
